# WorkBuddy actual usage record

Fill in only sessions that have already happened; do not pre-fill success records.

## Screenshot naming and the credential review

**Naming.** Screenshots use `YYYY-MM-DD-hanwei-NN-<slug>.png`, where `NN` continues that day's
sequence and `<slug>` names the session. A file still carrying the macOS default name
(`Screenshot <date> at <time>.png`) is renamed before it is committed.

**The credential rule.** **No screenshot enters this directory until it has been checked for an
`sk-` prefixed string or any other credential.** The repository is private today and will be made
public before submission, so a screenshot showing an API key on a console, settings or account page
would publish that key the moment the repository is flipped. Check the image itself, not only its
metadata: a byte scan of a PNG cannot see rendered text, so the image has to be opened and read.

| 2026-09-24 | Zhou Hanwei | Revise the judge prompt's limb amendment into an ordered procedure, after a read-only review found wording in it that could move cases the answer key has right today | `prompts/v1/judge.system.md`; `tests/test_pipeline.py`; `docs/notes/2026-09-24-the-limb-procedure-revised.md`; `docs/notes/2026-09-24-meeting-a-limb-and-the-dropped-record.md`; `runs/README.md` | The committed amendment was five paragraphs of principle, and each of the review's eight findings was the same failure in a different place: a sentence true of the confidence cap read as a rule about the limb. "It may carry a fact for the cap" read as "it may carry the limb" (so a driver's own messages lifted the cap on case 901, defeating the clause that makes an impaired trace the only evidence); "the test has not run" read as "the limb is not met" (so case 211, whose key is PARTIAL_REFUND 2.00, became a full refund through the conjunctive anchor clause); "a test the clause takes from another clause" read the wrong way (so case 214's summing rule became a borrowed test); "concedes against the speaker's own interest" let an admission replace a measured test; "where the artifact is missing the test has not run" turned an uncited traffic excuse into an untested limb and then an escalation, where 102, 105 and 114 all discard it under POL-RD-08; "stays a contradiction" was unqualified and could block the clause that resolves one, which 201 and 209 need; "this is not one of them" read as "the statement does not carry the fact" where POL-RD-07(ii) names it as corroboration; and the status sentence could make the tap and reason code that two limbs read look unproven. Each finding was verified against the policy files, the answer key and the case data before anything changed; all eight stand, and the one claim found wrong was this repository's own. The amendment is now an ordered procedure per limb — identify the test including any counting rule from another clause; is what it reads impaired over the stretch the defect covers; if not the result decides in either direction; if so the limb is undecided and moves no money either way — with the account, bridging, contradiction and counterparty rules following separately so none can be read as a step. 213 words added against `fb1af67`, none a digit, a case number, a place name or anything naming the sample; the file digest moves `d89552d0` to `6d15627e` and the prompts digest `8375d2bb` to `eece65ee`. | 2026-09-27 | Zhou Hanwei | Add an append-only, hash-chained event log to every run directory, and a per-case block of code-computed columns to the report | `tribunal/events.py`; `eval/events.py`; `tribunal/pipeline.py`; `eval/grade.py`; `eval/run_benchmark.py`; `eval/development_sets.py`; `tests/test_events.py`; `tests/test_grade.py`; `tests/test_run_benchmark.py`; `tests/test_development_sets.py`; `docs/notes/2026-09-27-the-event-log-and-the-report-columns.md`; `ui/dist/index.html`; `docs/buddy-log/README.md` | **No model call was made and no prompt was changed.** *The log.* `runs/<run directory>/events.jsonl` — one JSON object per line, ten event names (`invocation_start`, `invocation_end`, `case_start`, `agent_call`, `referee_decision`, `judge_draft`, `vote`, `ruling`, `case_failed`, `grade`), and every line carries its own context (`seq`, `ts`, `event`, `invocation`, `case`, `commit`, `prompts`, `prev`, `hash`) rather than a header at the top, because a log that named its invocation once could not answer the question after a second invocation appended. Append-only: nothing in `tribunal/events.py` truncates, rewrites or reorders, and **`--force` does not change that**; the tail is read off disk when the log opens, so a second invocation continues the first one's chain instead of restarting it; a lock wraps the read-modify-append because a run's cases are worked by several threads; every append is `fsync`ed. *Tamper evidence.* Each line records the sha256 of the previous line's canonical body — serialised through `money.dumps_canonical`, so the digest is over the exact bytes written — and its own. `python3 -m eval.events --verify` walks the chain and reports the **first** break, exiting non-zero: `content` (the line's own hash does not match its content — checked **first**, so an edit is reported at the line that was edited and not at the one after it), `chain` (`prev` does not name the previous line), `malformed`. The first line's `prev` is a sentinel of 64 zeros, which no digest can take, so a first line moved down the file cannot pass itself off as the beginning. *The two commands.* `python3 -m eval.events --case 211` prints a case's full history in order (`--json` prints the payloads through `money.dumps_canonical`); `--verify` checks the chain; `--path`, `--run-dir`, or `--model` with `--set` resolve which log. Both are read-only. *Live evidence, and it made no model call.* A **cached re-run** into a scratch copy (`cp -R runs/deepseek-deepseek-flash /tmp/evdemo/`, then `--runs-dir /tmp/evdemo`) reports **0 completed, 29 cached, 0 failed, 0 tokens charged, 6,239,134 reused** and wrote a **90-line** `events.jsonl`; `--verify` reports "90 line(s) verified … the chain is intact" and `--case 211` prints its three events in order. Only a material contradiction stops a draft.* The one live verifier call on record (case 101) shows the old rule was too strict: two drafts wrote `22.6%` where the brief's block carries `22.47%`, both abstained, and a unanimous **correct** ruling became `ESCALATE` although the rounding changed no conclusion (below the 90% test, refund above SGD 0.50 either way). Every `contradicted` verdict now carries `brief_value` and a **required boolean** `material` -- `isinstance(material, bool)` and not truthiness, because `isinstance(True, int)` is true and `"material": 1` does not say whether the difference matters -- and only a material contradiction stops its draft; a non-material one is recorded, flagged, and the draft keeps its vote. A `contradicted` verdict without a boolean `material` makes the whole answer unusable, exactly as before: no verification, every draft keeps its vote. `apply_verification(..., abstain_on=...)` takes `ABSTAIN_ON_MATERIAL` (the pipeline's rule) or `ABSTAIN_ON_ANY` (the old stricter reading), so one verdict set reads both ways with no second call; `verify_draft_statements`'s record gained `material`, `not_material` and `abstain_on`, and the `verification` event gained `material=` / `not_material=`. The prompt's worked example is **invented** -- `EV-DEMO-00042`, `EV-DEMO-00051`, `demo_totals.billable_units` -- and a search confirms those ids appear nowhere else in the repository or in either development set. *2. The prompt channel compares what the agents were given.* It read the cache entry's `prompts_sha256`, the digest of the whole prompt **directory**, so adding `verifier.system.md` staled all 44 cached results although no agent's instructions had changed. It now compares, **per role**, the `system_sha256` (and, for the advocates, `user_sha256`) that the recorded **calls** carry against what this code renders now, reusing `pipeline.compare_recorded_prompts` -- the same comparison `check_replay` makes for the advocates and `verify_drafts` makes for the judge. The path is a parameter defaulting to the repository's own, per rule 7. An entry carries eleven fields -- `precedent_id` (`P-0001`, ...), `source` (the set, the `case_no`, the `dispute_id`, the repository-relative run directory and the case file's sha256; the **set is derived from the run directory's own path**, because a schema-1 file has no `development_set`), `category`, `system_outcome`, `decision`, `clause_ids`, `amount_contested_sgd`, `summary` (at most 300 characters), `closed_by`, `policy_version` and `supersedes` -- and may carry no field name from `loader.FORBIDDEN_KEYS` nor `rationale`, `difficulty`, `governing_clauses`, `matched`, `expected` or `grade`; the walk is recursive and runs before anything is written. `close` reads five fields from a case file and no sixth, never reads `matched` and never reads `events.jsonl`, and takes the category, the dispute id and the contested amount from the **rebuilt brief**, so the answer-bearing half of a run is never opened. It refuses an absent case file, a brief that does not rebuild to `brief_sha256`, a ruling the loaded POL-GEN-03 `licenses` does not hold, `ESCALATE`, an amount that is not a decimal with at most two places, a clause or a referral's leading clause that does not resolve, and a second closure without `--supersedes`. The repository ships `precedents/README.md` and no entries. *What the judge sees.* `--precedents` is on both CLIs and off by default. When it is on and at least one precedent matches, the judge's user message carries a `precedents` key between `claims` and `referee`: a fixed note and up to three items of exactly four fields -- `precedent_id`, `category`, `clause_ids`, `ruling`. **No amount and no summary travel in the block.** When nothing matches the key is absent rather than empty, so the message is byte-for-byte the one a switch-off run builds, which a test asserts. The judge's system prompt is not edited. Whenever the key is present the parsed judge message is walked for a forbidden key at runtime, because the judge's message is a bundle boundary and the control is asserted at it rather than trusted to the library that wrote the entry. *The matching rule.* Seven steps, written down in `matches`'s docstring and exercised one at a time: the latest entry per source case; the same category; everything but the case being judged, by set and case number and separately by `dispute_id`; entries whose clause ids all resolve in this case's brief; entries sharing at least one clause with a claim the referee **kept**, scored by how many; ties by the relative amount difference in `Decimal` -- the difference over this case's contested amount, or the absolute difference when that is zero or null -- and then by the earlier entry; the top three. A clause reference is reduced to its base id before matching, so `POL-RD-02(a)` and `POL-RD-02` are one clause here. The choice is asserted to be the same under a shuffled store. *Runs and reporting.* A run with the block on writes to `runs/precedents/<model>/`, or `runs/codex/precedents/<model>/` for the second set, and `assert_precedents_destination` refuses a directory outside `<runs root>/precedents/`, seen to fail on a dirty fixture. `--precedents` is refused with `--ablate` (rule 6, one switch per invocation) and with `--replay-advocates`, `--replay-check`, `--verify-recorded-drafts` and `--external`, because every one of those reads or produces a run whose judge saw no block. `report.md` and the printed summary leave out every case that is the source of a library entry and name them, `invocation_end` records them, and both state that the figures are not comparable to the headline, because the library is built by people who have seen these development sets. *The third staleness channel.* A run records the library's chain head and the note's sha256 in its `configuration`, so a cached case whose recorded head is not the current one reads stale on a channel named `precedents` beside `brief` and `prompt`; the channel is silent rather than empty with the switch off, which keeps a plain run's staleness reading what it always was. The queue's line 426 says such a case "is stale and is re-run", but **no channel re-runs a stale cached case**: `eval/run_benchmark.py` turns every cached document into `STATUS_CACHED`, `--force` is the only re-run path, and three of this repository's own notes say reuse is deliberate. The reading was put to the owner and confirmed before the test was written -- this channel reports and reuses, and `--force` re-runs -- and the test asserts both halves, with the re-run shown to make no call and the forced run shown to make them. *Five corrections, and all five are the same mistake: a third channel added where only two were known.* (1) The runtime-boundary test spliced `matched` and expected the walk to refuse it, but the walk knows the five `loader.FORBIDDEN_KEYS` and `matched` is refused by the library at write time, not by the boundary; the fixture now splices `ground_truth`. (2) `_STALE_PHRASES` had no phrase for the third channel, so a library-driven staleness fell through to the fallback, `produced by an earlier version of this code` -- true of a moved brief and **false of a moved library, because the code did not move at all**; a phrase was added and a test now compares the whole set of `grade.MOVED_*` names against the table, **seen to go red** with the precedents phrase removed. | 2026-09-29 | Zhou Hanwei | Queue 2026-09-28 task 5: policy versions | `tribunal/policy_versions.py` (new: the version arithmetic, the amendment reader and `apply_amendments`); `tribunal/loader.py` (`load_policies(category, policy_version=1)`); `tribunal/brief.py` (`build_brief_for_case` only); `tribunal/pipeline.py`; `eval/run_benchmark.py`; `eval/development_sets.py`; `tests/test_policy_versions.py` (new, 52 tests); `policy/amendments/README.md` (new, and no amendments); `policy/proposals/README.md` (new, and no proposals); `runs/README.md`; `docs/notes/2026-09-29-the-policy-versions.md` | **No model call was made.** *What a version is.* An integer. Version 1 is the three files in `policy/` exactly as committed; version N is those files with the amendments `0001`..`N-1` from `policy/amendments/` applied, and `policy_versions.current_version()` is the one place `1 + len(files)` is computed. *Version 1 is byte-identical, and that is measured.* The queue's first test: version 1 rebuilds **all 44** recorded `brief_sha256` values exactly -- **29 of 29 corpus and 15 of 15 second set** -- against `runs/deepseek-deepseek-flash/` and `runs/codex/deepseek-deepseek-flash/`, and it is a test rather than a one-off. `load_policies` returns the two files as read for version 1, without a copy and without reading `policy/amendments/` at all, so a broken amendment cannot stop the version-1 measurement. *An amendment adds one clause, and cannot say otherwise.* The key set is compared for equality, so an `edits` or a `removes` key is a refusal and not an ignored extra, and the clause carries exactly the eight fields, so a ninth (`replaces`) is refused too: there is no document that could express an edit or a removal. The id must match `^POL-(NS\|RD\|GEN)-\d{2}$`, must be the **next free number** for its prefix, and its `category` must agree with the file its prefix names. A file not named `NNNN-<slug>.json`, a number used twice and a sequence with a hole are each refused; `README.md` is skipped. An amendment to a policy the brief in hand does not carry is skipped, not refused. *Threading the version.* `loader.load_policies` and `brief.build_brief_for_case` take it -- **not** `build_brief`, whose signature is pinned -- and it travels through `pipeline.run_case` and `run_loaded_case`, `run_benchmark.current_brief_sha256`, `brief_digest_for` and `BriefStaleness`, whose digest memo is keyed by case **and version together**. `ReplaySource` carries it, read from the source run's recorded `configuration`, because `replay_verdict` rebuilds the brief to compare against the recorded digest and rebuilding at version 1 would refuse every case of a run that was not. *Where a later version writes.* `runs/policy-v<N>/<model>/`, and `runs/codex/policy-v<N>/<model>/`; the destination is checked in code before anything is written, against **that version's** directory rather than `policy-v*`. `--policy-version N` is on both CLIs and is refused with `--ablate`, `--precedents`, `--compare`, `--replay-advocates`, `--replay-check`, `--verify-recorded-drafts` and `--external`; version 1 is the default, so naming it is not one of the switches. A version above the repository's is refused **before a client exists**, or the first case of such a run would have been the first report of it. *What finishing this task corrected.* **`--ablate` and `--precedents` never reached the runner on `eval.run_benchmark`'s command line** -- both were parsed and refused in combination, and neither was passed to `run_benchmark(...)`, so `--ablate clause-notes` built **unablated** briefs into `runs/<model>/`, exactly what `runs/README.md` and the destination assertion forbid; no test covered it, because the end-to-end tests drove `development_sets`, which forwards through `run_set(**kwargs)` and therefore worked. Both are now named at that call site beside `policy_version`, which had to be forwarded at the same line and would otherwise have been the only one of the three that worked. It is encoded as a test, so a later glob cannot arrive unnoticed. *What was run.* `tests/test_policy_versions.py` is new -- 52 tests over nine classes, covering the version arithmetic, the pinned prefix maps, every amendment refusal, the destination checks, the version-keyed staleness memo and both command lines. So this is not a tolerance the drafts exceeded — it is the shape of the check: on an upfront-quoted fare POL-RD-11(a)'s remedy is `charged_total` less the quote less the properly chargeable pass-throughs, a difference by construction. The money block was built around the development sets and **43 of 44 keys there read off it** (28/29 corpus, 15/15 second set; the one exception, corpus 109, is a prose-only POL-RD-04 figure the check skips by name). The published rule was **right** to refuse those three drafts — the 6.00 is a surcharge the fare ledger never billed (`EV-FARE-00501.airport_surcharge: 0.0`) — and the signed rule makes the subtraction readable only because the block does publish the 6.00. It was already a miss, so the measured figure does not move; what moves is that a case the system declined to decide is now decided, in the rider's disfavour, by 6.00. | 2026-10-01 | Zhou Hanwei | wild-v1 as dev: A2 — read a run under `runs/wild/` as the held-out set, and say when a report could analyse nothing at all — **no model call** | `tribunal/precedents.py`; `eval/policy_gaps.py`; `tests/test_policy_gaps.py`; `tests/test_precedents.py`; `docs/notes/2026-10-01-wild-runs-read-as-wild.md` (new) | **No model call was made.** Every command run is one the queue allows: `python3 -m unittest`, `python3 -m ui.build --check`, git, and read-only scans. *The cause.* `tribunal/precedents.py` derives a run's set from the run directory's own path, because a schema-1 case file carries no `development_set` field and a closure must still be able to say which set it came from — and the rule named exactly one directory, `codex`, with everything else falling through to `corpus`. `runs/wild/<model>/` was therefore read as the corpus, and two readers were wrong for it: `eval/policy_gaps.read_run` loaded every case through `development_set.load_case`, i.e. the **corpus manifest**, which does not list 501-533 or 9111-9114; and `precedents close` failed outright with `FATAL: case 501 is not listed in data/index.json`. *The fix.* One table, `EXTERNAL_SET_DIRS`, replaces the one-name rule: `codex` to `codex`, `wild` to `wild`, `wild-dev` to `wild` — because `wild` and `wild-dev` are two sets over one directory of cases, and naming the directory separately from the set is what lets them disagree on purpose. `set_from_run_directory` returns the component itself when it names a set in the table and `corpus` otherwise; `load_case_for_run` looks the set up in the same table and falls back to the manifest. Every other layout still reads as the corpus: `runs/precedents/<model>/`, `runs/ablation/<name>/<model>/`, `runs/policy-v2/<model>/`, `runs/amount-rule/<rule>/<model>/`. `eval/policy_gaps.py` keeps calling that one function, and **nothing from `eval/` is imported into `tribunal/`** — which leaves the table and the registry in `eval/development_sets.py` as two statements of one layout, a drift risk closed by `SetFromRunDirectoryTests`. *The symptom.* With the cause fixed, `policy_gaps report` over a wild run rebuilt no brief and then printed `No case in the given runs was left undecided, so there is no cluster to report` — a sentence that says the policy decided everything it was asked when the truth was that the report had no reading of the run at all. The two are now different sentences, chosen by `analysed_cases(run) == 0`: `nothing could be analysed` for any run that analysed zero cases for any reason, and `no case in the given runs was left undecided` for a run whose cases were read and none of which is a gap. The per-run line, the cluster line and stdout all use it. *The tests.* Three behavioural tests, each **seen to fail first** on the unfixed tree (`runs/wild-dev-a2-tests-fail-first.log`, exit 1): `AssertionError: 'wild' != 'corpus'`; `'nothing could be analysed' not found in ...` (the rendered report carried the other sentence); and `AssertionError: 0 != 1 : FATAL: case 501 is not listed in data/index.json`. The fixture is a run built by the rig under `runs/wild/<model-slug>/` over two of the held-out set's own cases and its own key, drafted through the scripted transport — a **temporary** run: nothing reads, writes or depends on the committed held-out run. `SetFromRunDirectoryTests` adds five tests over the derivation itself. *Modules run.* `tests.test_precedents`, `tests.test_policy_gaps`, `tests.test_policy_versions`, `tests.test_answer_isolation`, `tests.test_run_benchmark`, `tests.test_development_sets`, `tests.test_readme_figures`, `tests.test_baselines`, `tests.test_amount_rule`, `tests.test_ablation`, `tests.test_language`, `tests.test_upfront_remedy`, `tests.test_sealed_set`, `tests.test_policy_gaps_wild` — `Ran 679 tests in 3653.932s / OK (skipped=2)` over eleven of them and `Ran 202 tests in 1163.130s / OK` over the three that read `eval.policy_gaps` — and `python3 -m ui.build --check` passes. | - (none taken) | `d59bd2b`; this row in `df6c335` |

The block note says the figure assumes no rider-caused exoneration under `POL-RD-09(3)`, and that `crossed` is the reading of every key on the 13 upfront cases and is narrower than the clause's literal words. *Modules run.* `tests.test_upfront_remedy` — `Ran 14 tests in 72.015s / OK` — and the union run above, `Ran 679 tests in 3653.932s / OK (skipped=2)`. `python3 -m ui.build --check` passes. | - (none taken) | `e521d60`; this row in `df6c335` |

| 2026-10-03 | Zhou Hanwei | Sealed-run readiness: B — make the sealed retry protocol executable in `eval/development_sets.run_set` | `eval/development_sets.py`, `tests/test_sealed_set_wild_v2.py`, `docs/notes/2026-10-03-the-sealed-protocol-made-executable.md` | *The rule, fixed before the run.* The second freeze note's "retried once with the same command" and "a `SHAPE` failure is not retried" could not both be followed with the code as it stood: `load_cached` and `_persist` keep a case file only for a success and nothing read `failure_kind`, so a second invocation re-attempted every case with no `case-<NNNNN>.json` — which is what `wild-v1`'s second invocation did to 517 and 533, and it overwrote their first transcripts. The rule is now executable: a sealed set with `force` false reads its own `events.jsonl` with `tribunal.events.read_events` (read-only) and drops the cases the protocol does not retry, printing `not retried under the sealed protocol: <case_no> (<reason>)` with the reason `SHAPE, every call answered 200` or `two attempts`, and printing an attempt that ended with no terminal event so the record shows it. `force` is untouched, `--force` is still refused for a sealed set, and nothing new is imported into `tribunal/`. *One place the code had to go past the literal wording.* The rule defines an attempt by its `agent_call` events, but a transport failure raises inside `LLMClient.complete` before the transcript entry exists, so such an invocation records a `case_failed` and no `agent_call`; under the literal definition the two-attempt cap could never bind on `TRANSPORT`, `TIMEOUT`, `DEADLINE` or `INTERRUPTED`, and a case failing that way would be retried forever. The executable rule counts an invocation as an attempt when it has an `agent_call` **or** a terminal event for the case. Stated in the note and beside the code. *Seen to fail first.* `SealedProtocolTests` (7 tests) was run against the code with each line of B2 removed in turn: with the call site in `run_set` removed, **5 of 7** went red (`Ran 7 tests / FAILED (failures=5)`); with the `all(status == HTTP_OK ...)` clause removed, **3** went red (`FAILED (failures=3)`) — the two `SHAPE` tests and the predicate test; with the two-attempt cap removed, **3** went red (`FAILED (failures=3)`) — the gateway test, the transport test and the predicate test. | 2026-10-03 | Zhou Hanwei | Sealed-run readiness: D2 — one read-only command that prints a sealed set's figures from its committed run | `eval/sealed_report.py` (new); `tests/test_sealed_report.py` (new) | *Which command, and why.* The sheet offered `python3 -m eval.development_sets --report --set wild-v2` or `python3 -m eval.sealed_report --set <name>`, and the second is the one built. `render_development_sets` is the development sets' report: it prints the corpus base rate beside the figures it computes, and a sealed set's figures printed there would stand beside a target they are not answerable to; suppressing it would mean a second mode inside a report whose whole point is the development figures. `eval/sealed_report` is about sealed sets only, so it cannot print the corpus target by accident. No corpus target: the test asserts that neither `TARGET_ESCALATION_RATE` nor the word `target` appears. *A set that has not been run prints one line and nothing else*, and the emptiness check is made **before** the key is loaded, so the not-run path reads no key at all — which is what the sealed-content rule requires of `wild-v2`. The test patches `grade.load_answer_key` to raise and asserts the **whole** output string, so a command that read the key on that path would fail there rather than quietly doing what the rule forbids. *The held-out set's figures, reproduced.* Against the committed `runs/wild/` the command prints 55/62 and 55/64, ruling 56/62 and 56/64, `ROUTE_DEVIATION` 30/35 over produced and 30/37 over all, `NO_SHOW_CHARGE` 25/27 on both bases, precision 3/7 and recall 3/4, and 517 and 533 each with `last failure kind SHAPE` — every figure `docs/notes/2026-09-30-the-sealed-run.md` states. The note is unchanged; a figure that differed would be reported, not adjusted. *Seen to fail first.* Each guard was removed in turn and the module restored byte-identically (`restored: True`): the not-run path made to load the key before the emptiness check → the not-run test `FAILED (failures=1)`, the other four `OK`; the over-all basis made the over-produced one → the figures test `FAILED (failures=4)`, the rest `OK`; the corpus target added to the escalation line → the no-target test `FAILED (failures=1)`, the rest `OK`; the no-ruling list reduced to bare case numbers → the failure-kind test `FAILED (failures=1)`, the rest `OK`; the sealed-name check removed → the refusal test `FAILED (failures=1)`, the rest `OK`. The first attempt at the third mutation added a `.format()` argument without its placeholder and changed nothing, so the test stayed green; that is recorded rather than tidied away, because a mutation that cannot fire is exactly what the red proof is for. It was corrected and re-run — `notarget FAILED (failures=1)`, `figures OK` — before this row was written. *Tests.* `tests.test_sealed_report` `Ran 5 tests in 26.279s / OK`. | - (none taken) | `5925245` |

| 2026-10-05 | Zhou Hanwei | Viewer fixes: 2 — the wild page's token figures: `run.json`'s `tokens.failed` counts only the invocation that *retried* each failed case, so the page stated 526,237 for the two cases that produced no ruling when they cost 992,266, and a run total of 13,069,994 where the run spent 13,536,023; the figure is now summed from the run's `events.jsonl` across every invocation, the page says which sum it is, and the test recomputes both sums instead of pinning a number | `ui/build.py`; `tests/test_ui_build.py`; `ui/dist/index.html`; `ui/dist/codex/index.html`; `ui/dist/wild/index.html`; `ui/dist/wild-v2/index.html`; `ui/dist/ryde-901/index.html`; `docs/buddy-log/README.md` | **No model call was made and no browser was launched.** *The defect, in two halves.* The card read `run.json`'s `tokens.failed.total_tokens` and added it to the sum over the case records. The case records carry only the cases that produced a ruling, so the two that did not contribute nothing from that side — which is why the failure total was added at all — and `tokens.failed` is the **retry** invocation's own consumption. So the page stated 13,069,994 for a run that spent 13,536,023 — and `run.json`'s `total_for_the_case_set` is 13,069,994 as well, which is why the page and the run file agreed with each other and both were low. The arithmetic is exact: 13,069,994 + (992,266 − 526,237) = 13,536,023. *The fix.* `ui/build.py` gains `_agent_call_tokens`, which reads the run's `events.jsonl` a line at a time and sums `total_tokens` per case over every `agent_call` entry it carries; the failed cases' figure is that sum, so the page's total is now the sum over the whole log, which for this run is the 13,536,023 `docs/notes/2026-09-30-the-sealed-run.md` records and the note's 992,266 for the pair. The log is read only when the run has failures, so a fixture run directory that has none still builds; and a failed case the log never names raises `BuildError` rather than quietly adding zero. The card says which sum it is — `summed from the run's event log (events.jsonl) across every invocation` — and the data block carries `failed_source` for the same reason. *The test.* `test_the_failed_cases_tokens_are_in_the_total` no longer pins 526237: it recomputes both sums from `events.jsonl` with its own reader and asserts the page's `failed_total` and `total` against them, red first with `AssertionError: 992266 != 526237`, and it asserts the run's own failure total is **smaller** than the right one, which states the defect as a property rather than as a number. | 2026-10-08 | Zhou Hanwei | Policy v3: 2 — the code version 3's clauses rely on. Every change is gated behind `policy_version >= 3`, and versions 1 and 2 stay byte-identical. **Item 1, money and dwell.** `tribunal/money.py` gained `fee_in_force`, `gross_fee_in_force`, `free_wait_in_force` and `waiting_fee_in_force`, which read the block, and `fee_arithmetic` gained a `gross_fee`, so POL-NS-08(d) is satisfied by a `fare_breakdown` line that equals the net **or** the gross. In `tribunal/brief.py`, POL-NS-01(c)'s free waiting period — and so POL-NS-05(i)'s and POL-NS-06's, which both read POL-NS-01(c) — comes from `platform_policy_in_force`; the no-show fee comes from the block, with `fee_schedule.defaults.cancellation_fee_sgd` and then the version-1 greater-of as fallbacks; POL-NS-10(d)'s waiting-time fee is published in the money block's constants; and `schedule_fee_dimension` is not published at version 3, because the block states one fee rather than choosing between two tables. **Item 2, integrity weights.** `prepass.weight_for` takes a `weights` mapping and `weights_from_policy` reads `integrity.weights`; `gaps_from_points` computes a trace's gaps from its own points, and `gps_gap_variant` grades each `GPS_GAP` into POL-NS-08(b)'s three variants — at most 120 s or outside the waiting window is `GPS_GAP_SHORT_OR_OUTSIDE_WINDOW`, a longer gap overlapping the window is `GPS_GAP_LONG_INSIDE_WINDOW`, and one containing the cancellation is `GPS_GAP_STRADDLING_CANCELLATION`. The waiting window is the one POL-NS-01(c) measures dwell over, from the arrival marker to the cancellation, so a completed trip has none. At version 3 the brief's `admissibility` carries the variant code, the recomputed `weight_multiplier`, the variant on the matching `issue_details` entry and `recomputed_at_policy_version`; the case file's own `admissibility` is never edited. **Item 3, case 901.** `eval/external/ryde/convert.py` gained `--policy-version`; version 3 maps the sample's `cancellation_policy` to `trip_record.platform_policy` (source `RECORD-PLATFORM`), its `scheduled_time` to `scheduled_at` and its ten `app_events` to `events[]`, with the driver's name and the rider's payment method redacted from two details, and writes `data/external/ryde-v3/case-00901.json` with its own `mapping.json`. **Item 4, scope gates.** `dispute.safety_flag`, `dispute.complaint_flag`, `trip_record.scheduled_at` and `trip_record.events[]` are optional and no case in this repository carries any of them, so absent means false or null and no code had to be added: a case without them briefs at version 3 without error, and a test holds it. **Item 5.** `docs/notes/2026-10-08-policy-version-3.md` written, with the `GPS_GAP` table. No case file under `data/cases` or under an existing `data/external` set changed, no answer key, no prompt and no `policy/*.json` changed; the one new case file is `data/external/ryde-v3/case-00901.json`. | 2026-10-11 | Zhou Hanwei | Docs: 43 — the four weaknesses an independent review confirmed on 2026-10-11, disclosed in the two places they must be and **not fixed**, because `tribunal/`, `prompts/` and `policy/` do not change again before the submission. **(1) The confidence floor fails open.** `tribunal/pipeline.py` applies the 0.70 floor only when a draft's `confidence` is a JSON number — `_as_number` returns `None` for a missing key, `null`, a boolean or a non-numeric string, and the gate is `confidence is not None and confidence < floor` — so such a draft is not held to the floor at all and votes like any other, and a confidence written as the string `"NaN"` reaches `Decimal("NaN") < floor` and raises `decimal.InvalidOperation` inside the check instead of abstaining. **(2) No retry on a transport error.** `tribunal/llm.py` retries only `RETRY_STATUSES = (429, 500, 502, 503, 504)`; a read timeout, a connection reset or an endpoint that does not resolve fails the case on its **first** attempt — `_urllib_transport` raises `TransportError` for a `URLError` and lets a timeout propagate — and an HTTP 4xx is handed back as an ordinary result, so the runner records it as an unusable answer rather than as an HTTP error. **(3) A case whose pipeline fails is not queued for a person.** `eval/run_benchmark.py`'s `_persist` writes a case file only for a case that succeeded and `ops/queue.py`'s `disputes_from_run` builds the queue by globbing `case-*.json`, so a failed dispute is absent from the queue rather than routed to the human lane and must be re-run. **(4) The default policy version is 1.** `eval/run_benchmark.DEFAULT_POLICY_VERSION` is 1 and `eval.development_sets`' registration default is 1, while the submitted system is version 6 — so every README command that is meant to reproduce a version-6 figure was checked, and each already passes `--policy-version 6` (the five version-6 development commands, the one-case example and the sample-case command);
